          Period (T)
          ld,dj¾;h

          Period is the time taken for one complete wave
          cycle to pass a point.
          ld,dj¾;h hkq tla iïmQ¾K ;rx. pl%hla i|yd
          ,laIHhla miq lsÍug .;jk ld,hhs'

          It's the inverse of frequency.
          th ixLHd;fha m%;sf,dauh fõ'

          T = 1/f
          T - Period
          F - Frequency

          Period and frequency are opposites: when the
          period increases, the frequency decreases; when             In-Phase Waves: When two waves have the same
          the period decreases, the frequency increases.              phase, their peaks and troughs align, resulting in
          wdj¾; ld,h iy ixLHd;h hkq tlsfklg m%;sf,dau                 constructive interference, where the amplitude
          idOl fõ( wdj¾; ld,h jeä jk úg ixLHd;h wvq                   is increased.
          fõ¦ wdj¾; ld,h wvq jk úg                                    ;rx. follg tlu l,djla we;s úg" tAjdfha YS¾I
          ixLHd;h jeä fõ'                                             iy ksïk iumd; jk w;r" tys m%;sM,hla f,i
                                                                      constructive interference we;s fõ" tys§ úia;drh
          If one wave cycle takes 2 seconds, the period is 2          jeä fõ'
          s, so the frequency is low. If one wave cycle takes
          0.5 seconds, the period is 0.5 s, so the frequency          Example:
          is high.                                                    When two speakers emit identical sound waves in
          hï ;rx. pl%hla i|yd ;;amr 2 la .;jkafka kï"                 phase, their peaks add together to create
          tys wdj¾; ld,h ;;amr 2 ls ^T = 2s)' túg tys                 increased amplitude and louder volume.

          Circular Motion to Waveform
          jD;a; p,s;fhka ;rx. wdldrhla idod .ekSu

          Consider a point moving around a circle with a
          constant angular velocity.
          ksh; flda‚l m%fõ.hlska jD;a;hla jgd .uka lrk
          ,laIHhla i,ld n,kak'

          As it moves, its position can be described by two
          components: one along the x-axis (horizontal) and
          one along the y-axis (vertical).
          th p,kh jk úg" tys msysàu ixrpl follska
          úia;r l< yelsh( tlla x-wlaIh Tiafia ^;sria& iy
          wfkl y-wlaIh ^isria& Tiafia'

          If we project the position of this point onto the x-
          axis, the vertical displacement (y-component) of
          the point over time forms a sine wave.
          fuu ,laIHfha msysàu x-wlaIhg m%lafIamKh
          lrfyd;a" ld,h;a iuÕ ,laIHfha isria úia:dmkh
          ^y-ixrplh& ihskdldr ;rx.hla idohs'

          The horizontal displacement corresponds to time,
          and the vertical displacement corresponds to the
          wave's amplitude.
          ;sria úia:dmkh ld,hg wkqrEm jk w;r isria
          úia:dmkh ;rx.fha úia;drhg wkqrEm fõ'

          The angle of the point in the circular motion
          corresponds to the phase of the wave.
          jD;a; p,s;fha fhfok ,laIHfha fldaKh ;rx.fha
          l,djg wkqrEm fõ'

          f;l,d

          f;l,d n,h                                                  ix{d iy ;rx. w;r fjki

          In a three-phase system, there are three sine              Waves
          waves (each phase) offset by 120 degrees from              A wave is simply a physical phenomenon that
          each other.                                                exists around us all the time.
          f;l,d moaO;shl" ihskdldr ;rx. ;=kla ^tla tla               ;rx.hla hkq ir,ju wm jgd iEu úgu mj;sk
          l,dj& tlsfklska wxYl ^120& lska úia:dmkh fõ'               fN!;sl ixisoaêhls'

          Because of this phase offset, at least one phase is        Waves can be found everywhere in nature, such as
          always at or near its peak voltage while the others        light waves from the sun, sound waves in the air,
          are crossing zero.                                         or ocean waves. However, not all waves carry

                                                                     Let’s say you were to download a video with a
          As an analogy, we can use an illustration of               size of 4GB using two internet connections, one
          water flowing through two pipes.                           with 100Mbps and the other with 500Mbps but
          WodyrKhla f,i" k< folla yryd .,d hk c,h

          how much water can flow through the pipes.

          we;'                                                       To calculate the amount of time it takes to

          Let’s assume that water flow at the same rate              the bandwidth and the video size into one unit.
          (speed) in both the pipes.                                 ùäfhdaj nd.; lsÍug .;jk ld,h .Kkh lsÍu
          k< foflysu tlu wkqmd;hlska ^fõ.fhka& c,h                   i|yd" uq,skau l,dm m<, iy ùäfhda mgfha
          .,d hkafk hehs is;uq'                                      m%udKh tla tAllhlg mßj¾;kh l< hq;=h'

                                                                     100Mbps / 8 = 12.5MBps
                                                                     500Mbps / 8 = 62.5MBps

                                                                     4GB = 4096MB

                                                                     T100mbps = 4096MB / 12.5MBps
                                                                     T100mbps = 4096MB / 12.5MBps
                                                                     T100mbps = 327.68 seconds
                                                                     T100mbps = 327.68 seconds / 60
                                                                     T100mbps = 5.46 minutes

                                                                     T500mbps = 4096MB / 62.5MBps
                                                                     T500mbps = 4096MB / 62.5MBps
                                                                     T500mbps = 65.536 seconds
          But since the bottom pipe is wider than the top
                                                                     T500mbps = 65.536 seconds / 60
          one, it is able to flow a lot more water at the            T500mbps = 1.09 minutes
          same time, more than the top pipe.
          kuq;a my< k<h by< k<hg jvd m<, neúka"
          by< k<hg jvd jeä c, m%udKhla tljr .,d                      So, bandwidth increases the amount of data
          hdug thg yelshdj we;'                                      that can be sent per second. A higher
                                                                     bandwidth connection (500 Mbps) allows more
          So, the wider pipe will fill up the container a lot        bits to be transmitted simultaneously compared
          faster than the smaller pipe.                              to a lower bandwidth connection (100 Mbps).
          tneúka" mq¿,a k<h l=vd k<hg jvd jeä                        tneúka" l,dm m<, u.ska ;;amrhlg heúh yels
          fõ.fhka Ndckh mqrjkq we;'                                  o;a; m%udKh jeä lrhs' by< l,dm m<,
                                                                     iïnkaO;djhla u.ska ^500 Mbps& wvq l,dm

                                                                     ìgq iïfm%aIKh lsÍug bv i,ihs'

          Routing and Switching                                  Examples
          Each router or switch along the path takes a
          small processing delay, which can add up across        High bandwidth + High latency

          Synchronization

          ksrka;rj isÿjk ixl%dka;s iïfm%aIlh iuÕ .%dylh
          iuuqyq¾; lsÍu myiq lrhs'

          Error Detection

                                                                      iEu úgu ixl%dka;shla mj;sk neúka" iudk ìgq j,
          errors, as missing or extra transitions would               È.= wkqms<sfj,la iuÕ jqjo" .%dylhdg hjkakd iuÕ
          indicate a problem.                                         wLKavj iuuqyq¾; l< yel'
          ke;sùï fyda wu;r ixl%dka;s u.ska .eg¨jla
          fmkakqï lrk neúka" ix{dfõ ksrka;r fjkiaùï

          fodaI y÷kd .ekSug WmldÍ fõ'

          Disadvantages

          1. Amplitude Modulation (AM)
             úia;dr uQ¾ckh

          In amplitude modulation, the amplitude of the
          carrier signal is varied in proportion to the
          amplitude of the information (baseband) signal,
          while the frequency and phase of the carrier
          remain constant.
          úia;drh uqyq¾ckfha§" jdyl ix{dfõ úia;drh

          ksh;j mj;S'

          by a low carrier amplitude, while a high-
          amplitude message signal is represented by a
          high carrier amplitude

          Quantizing
          Quantizing is the process of converting the
          sampled analog values into a finite set of levels.
          Quantizing hkq ksheÈ ,nd.;a m%;siu w.hka iSñ;
          uÜgï lÜg,hla njg mßj¾;kh lsÍfï
          l%shdj,shhs'

          This step maps the infinite range of amplitudes of
          the analog signal to a limited number of discrete
          values.
          fuu mshjr u.ska m%;siu ix{dfõ wiSñ; mrdih
          iSñ; úúla; w.hka .Kklg is;shï.; lrhs'

          The more levels available, the more accurately the          Encoding
          signal can be represented.                                  Encoding is the process of converting each
          mj;sk uÜgï jeä jk ;rug ix{dj jvd;a                          quantized sample into a binary number.
          ksjerÈj ksrEmKh l< yelsh'                                   Encoding hkq tla tla m%udKd;aul idïm, oaúuh
                                                                      ixLHdjla njg mßj¾;kh lsÍfï l%shdj,shhs'
          Example
          If we have 8 bits for each sample, we can have 2 8          Each level from the quantization process is
          = 256 possible levels to represent the amplitude.           represented by a binary code, which is then
          wmg tla tla ksheÈh i|yd ìgq 8la ;sfí kï"                    transmitted or stored.
          úia;drh ksrEmKh lsÍug yels uÜgï 28 } 256la
          ;sìh yel'                                                   Quantization l%shdj,sfha iEu uÜgulau oaúuh
                                                                      fla;hlska ksrEmKh jk w;r th iïfm%aIKh fyda
                                                                      .nvd lrkq ,efí'
          With 16 bits, we have 216 = 65,536 levels, which
          leads to a more accurate representation of the
                                                                      Example
          signal.

          Data transmission modes                               Hubs
          o;a; iïmafraIK úê                                     Hubs function in half-duplex mode. This means
          Data transmission modes refer to the ways in          that data can only travel in one direction at a
          which data is transmitted between two devices         time across the network
          in a communication system.                            WodyrKhla f,i" yí tllg iïnkaO tla
          o;a; iïfm%aIK úê hkq ikaksfõok moaO;shl               Wmdx.hla o;a; hjkafka kï" wfkla ish¨u
          Wmdx. folla w;r o;a; iïfm%aIKh jk                     Wmdx. ;ukaf.a o;a; heùug fmr iïfm%aIKh
          wdldrhhs'                                             iïmQ¾K jk f;la /£ isáh hq;=h'

          There are three data transmission modes.              If one device connected to a hub is sending
          uQ,sl o;a; iïfm%Ia K l%u ;=kla we;'                   data, all other devices must wait until the
                                                                transmission is complete before they can send
          1.     Simplex / tAl m:                               their own data.
          2.     Half-Duplex / w¾O oaùm:                        kdì w¾O oaùm: l%ufhka l%shd lrhs' fuhska woyia
          3.     Full-Duplex / mQ¾K oaùm:                       lrkafka o;a; cd,h yryd .uka l< yelafla tla
                                                                ÈYdjlg muKla njhs'
          1. Simplex transmission
          tAl m: iïfm%IKh                                       Fax machines
                                                                When two fax machines communicate, one
          In simplex mode, data transmission occurs in          machine sends data (the scanned document)

                                                                   msßyS hhs'

          How an NIC works
          NIC tll l%shdldÍ;ajh

          from a DHCP server.

          fodrgq uÕ iy rjqgrh w;r fjki'

          •      Although a router and a gateway are different devices, a router often acts as a gateway in home
                 and office networks.
                 rjqgrhla iy fodrgq uÕla hkq tlsfklg fjkia Wmdx. folla jqjo" ksjdi iy ld¾hd,Sh cd,hkays
                 rjqgrhla fndfyda úg fodrgq uÕla f,i l%shd lrhs'

                 ish¨u rjqgr fodrgq ux jk kuq;a" tajdg wNHka;r cd, w;r o;a; ud¾..; lsÍu o isÿ l< yelsh'

          Types of Hybrid Topologies
          fouqyqï ia:, j¾.

          Star-Bus Topology
          Multiple star networks connected through a
          common bus backbone.
          fmdÿ nia m%Odk ikaksfõok flan,hla yryd
          tlsfklg iïnkaO lrk ,o ;re cd, lsysmhl
          tl;=jls'

          Star-Ring Topology
          Several star networks connected in a ring for
          better reliability and management.
          jvd fyd| úYajdijka;Ndjhla iy
          l<ukdlrKhla i|yd jguh wdldrhg tlsfkl
          iïnkaO lrk ,o ;re cd, lsysmhls'

          Star-Mesh Topology
          Star networks interconnected using mesh links
          to provide high redundancy and fault tolerance.
          by< w;sßla;;djla iy fodaI bjiSfï yelshdjla
          ,nd §u i|yd oe,siuh ine¢ Ndú;fhka
          tlsfklg iïnkaO lrk ,o ;re cd, fõ'

          Bus-Ring Topology
          Combines a bus backbone with a ring structure
          to connect different network segments.
          úúO cd, fldgia tlsfklg iïnkaO lsÍu i|yd
          nia m%Odk flan,hla iy jguh jHqyhla tlg
          tl;= lr ks¾udKh lrk ,oaols'

          Tree-Star Topology
          Multiple star networks connected in a
          hierarchical tree structure using core and
          distribution switches.
          m%Odk iy fnodyeÍfï iaúphka Ndú;d lrñka"
          Oqrdj,s jDlaI jHqyhlg tlsfklg iïnkaO lrk
          ,o ;re cd, lsysmhls'

          Tree-Mesh Topology
          A tree topology enhanced with mesh
          connections between key switches for
          redundancy.
          w;sßla;;dj i|yd m%Odk iaúphka w;r oe,siuh
          ine¢ tla lrñka ;jÿrg;a ÈhqKq lrk ,o
          jDlaI cd, jHqyhls'

          Snowflake Topology
          An extended star (hierarchical) topology where
          secondary devices connect to additional
          devices, forming a snowflake-like structure.
          oaú;Shsl Wmdx. ;j;a w;sf¾l Wmdx. iuÕ
          iïnkaO fjñka" ysu msh,a,l yevh .kakd mßÈ
          úys§ hk" ÈhqKq lrk ,o ;re cd, jHqyhls'

          Central device / uOHu Wmdx.h                             Star-Based Tree Topology
          No single central switch for the entire network.         (Hierarchical Switched Tree)

          individual branches to the backbone.

          Scalability / mßudKH;dj
          Moderate. New branches can be added by
          connecting another hub or switch to the
          backbone, but excessive expansion can reduce
          network performance.

          The satellite dish sends the request as radio             Satellite internet is less affected by local

          ieg,hsÜ wekagkdj uÕska tu b,a,Su f¾äfhda                  outages. As long as the dish has a clear view of

          ieg,hsÜ tlg tu ix{dj ,efnk w;r" th

           Feature                     NAT                  Proxy                    VPN

           OSI Layer                   L3                   L7                       L3/L2

          HTTP Status Codes                                            FTP (File Transfer Protocol)
          Servers respond to requests with status codes that
          indicate the outcome.                                        A standard network protocol used for transferring
          m%;sM,h fmkakqï lrk ;;aj fla; iuÕska                         files between a client and a server.
          b,a,Sïj,g fiajdodhlhka m%;spdr olajhs                        fiajd,dNsfhl= iy fiajdodhlfhl= w;r f.dkq

          Collisions
          Because     devices    send    data     without
          coordination, two or more devices might send
          data simultaneously, causing a collision. When
          this happens, the data gets garbled and needs
          to be resent.
          Wmdx. iïnkaëlrKhlska f;drj o;a; hjk
          ksid" Wmdx. folla fyda jeä .Kkla tljr o;a;
          heúh yels w;r" tu.ska .egqula we;s lrhs' fuh
          isÿ jQ úg" o;a; fjkia jk w;r kej; heùug
          isÿ fõ'

          Acknowledgment
          The sender waits for an acknowledgment from
          the receiver that the data was received
          successfully. If no acknowledgment is received,
          it assumes a collision occurred and resends the
          data after a random waiting time.
          hjkakd o;a; id¾:lj ,enqKq njg ,nkakdf.ka
          ms<s.ekSula n,dfmdfrd;a;=fjka isà' ms<s.ekSula
          fkd,enqfka kï" th .egqula isÿ jQ nj Wml,amkh
          lrk w;r wyUq /£ isàfï ld,hlska miqj o;a;
          kej; hjhs'

          Randomized Retransmission
          After a collision, devices wait for a random time
          before retransmitting. This reduces the chance
          of repeated collisions.                                   Pure ALOHA has a relatively low efficiency because
          .egqulska miq" Wmdx. kej; iïfm%I     a Kh lsÍug           of frequent collisions.

          Code division multiplexing (CDM)                            Applications of CDM
          fla;h fnod .ekSfï nyqm:lrKh                                 CDM ys fhÿï
          In CDM, multiple data signals are transmitted
          over the same frequency band at the same time,              CDMA cellular networks
          but each signal is encoded with a unique code.              CDM is widely used in Code Division Multiple
          CDM ys" nyq o;a; ix{d tlu ixLHd; l,dmhla                    Access (CDMA) cellular networks.
          yryd tljr iïfm%I       a Kh fõ" kuq;a iEu                   CDM nyq,j Ndú;d jkafka Code Division
          ix{djlau wkkH fla;hlska fla;kh lr we;                       Multiple Access (CDMA) fi,shq,¾ cd, j,hs'

          This process spreads the signal across a wider              Wireless local area networks (WLANs) CDM
          frequency band.                                             can be used in WLANs to improve capacity and
          fuu l%shdj,sh mq¿,a ixLHd; l,dmhla yryd

          Confidentiality                                          Cryptography is the process of hiding or coding
          ryiHNdjh                                                 information so that only the person a message
                                                                   was intended for can read it.
          Confidentiality ensures that information is only         .=ma;fla;kh hkq m‚úvhla woyia lrk
          accessible to those authorized to view it.               mqoa.,hdg muKla th lshúh yels jk f,i

          th ne,Sug n,h ,;a whg muKla nj iy;sl
          lrhs'                                                    Encryption and Decryption

          Without confidentiality, sensitive data could be
          intercepted and read by unauthorized parties,            Encryption is the process of converting
          leading to potential misuse or loss of privacy.          plaintext (readable data) into ciphertext
          ryiHNdjhlska f;drj" wkjir md¾Yajhka úiska                (encoded data) to protect it from unauthorized
          ixfõ§ o;a; .%yKh lr lshùug yels jk w;r"                  access.
          tuÕska wksis Ndú;hg fyda fm!oa.,sl;ajh wysñ              fla;kh hkq ir, fm< ^lshúh yels o;a;&
          ùug fya;= úh yel'                                        wkjir m%fõYfhka wdrlaId lsÍu i|yd fla;s;

          For instance, if personal health records or              l%shdj,shhs'

          they could be accessed and exploited by                  Decryption is the reverse process, converting

          ksoiqkla jYfhka" mqoa.,sl fi!LH jd¾;d fyda               users can read it.

          Authentication
          i;Hdmkh

          Authentication verifies the identity of the
          sender or the source of information.
          i;Hdmkh hjkakdf.a wkkH;djh fyda f;dr;=re
          uQ,dY%h i;Hdmkh lrhs'

          It ensures that the message or data comes from
          a legitimate and trusted source, preventing
          impersonation or fraudulent communications.
          mqoa.,dfrdamKh fyda jxpksl ikaksfõokhka                  Cryptographic Keys
          j<lajñka m‚úvh fyda o;a; kS;Hdkql+, iy                   .=ma;fla;k h;=re
          úYajdiodhl uQ,dY%hlska meñfKk nj iy;sl
          lrhs'                                                    Keys are secret pieces of information used in
                                                                   encryption and decryption. The security of the

          Media Access Control (MAC) Address

          A MAC Address is a unique physical address
          assigned to a device's Network Interface Card
          (NIC) by the manufacturer. It is used to identify
          devices and enable communication within a
          Local Area Network (LAN).
          MAC ,smskhla hkq Wmdx.hl ksIamdolhd úiska
          tys we;s cd, w;=reuqyqK;a ldâm;g ,nd fok
          iqúfYaI"S fN!;sl ,smskhls' fuh ia:dkSh cd,hla
          ;=< Wmdx. y÷kd .ekSug iy tAjd w;r
          ikaksfõokh isÿ lsÍug Ndú;d lrhs'

          MAC addresses operate at the Data Link Layer
          (Layer 2) of the OSI Model.
          MAC ,smsk" OSI wdlD;sfha o;a; iïnkaOl ia;rh
          ;=< l%shd;aul fõ'

          Private IP Address                                        Static IP Address
          mqoa.,sl IP ,smskh                                        ia:djr IP ,smskh

          A Private IP Address is an IP address used within         A Static IP Address is a permanent IP address

          Network Address Translation (NAT)
          cd, ,smk
                 s mßj¾;kh

          Network Address Translation (NAT) is a technique
          used by routers to translate private IP addresses
          used within a local network into a public IP
          address used on the Internet, and vice versa.
          cd, ,smks mßj¾;kh hkq ia:dkSh cd,hla ;=<
          Ndú;d jk mqoa.,sl IP ,smk s " wka;¾cd,h ;=< Ndú;d

          lrkq ,nk ;dlaIKhls'

          the Internet.

          CIDR notation                                             Example 01
          (Classless Inter-Domain Routing)
                                                                    192.168.10.45/27
          CIDR notation is a compact way to represent an
          IP address along with its associated network              Network mask ⟶ 255.255.255.224
          prefix or default mask.
          CIDR wxlkh hkq cd, Wmi¾.h fyda iïu;
          wdjrKh iuÕ IP ,smskhla ksfhdackh lsÍug
          Ndú;d lrk ixhqla; l%uhls'

          It consists of the IP address followed by a slash         Network address = 192.168.10.32
          (/) and a number that represents the number of            Broadcasting address = 192.168.10.63
          bits used for the block part of the address.
          th IP ,smskfhka miqj slash ^$& tlla iy ,smskfha           Usable minimum IP = 192.168.10.33       30
          ldKav fldgi i|yd Ndú;d lrk ìgq .Kk                                                                devices
                                                                    Usable maximum IP = 192.168.10.62
          ksfhdackh lrk wxlhlska iukaú; fõ'

          IP block                                                  Example 02
          IP ldKavh
                                                                    172.20.150.200/20
          IP block refers to the network part and cannot
          be changed.                                               Network mask ⟶ 255.255.255.224
          ldKavh hkq cd, fldgi f,i yeÈkaúh yels w;r
          th fjkia l< fkdyel'

          The number after the slash (Ex. /26) indicates

          DMZ (Demilitarized Zone) / hqOuqla; l,dm                    There are 2 ways to implement a DMZ
                                                                      DMZ tlla l%shd;aul lsÍug l%u 2 la we;
          A DMZ is a separate network segment (subnet)
          placed between an internal (trusted) network                1. Single firewall (three-legged firewall)
          and an external (untrusted) network, usually the
          internet.
          DMZ hkq wNHka;r ^úYajdiodhl& cd,hla iy
          ndysr ^wúYajdiodhl& cd,hla ^idudkHfhka
          wka;¾cd,h& w;r msysgj    q d we;s fjku cd,
          fldgils ^Wmcd,hls&'

          It hosts servers that need to be reachable from
          outside, without exposing the internal LAN
          directly.
          wNHka;r LAN cd,h iDcqju ksrdjrKh fkdlr"
          ndysßka m%fõY úh hq;= fiajdodhlhka fuys r|jd
          ;nd .kS'
                                                                      Cheaper, simpler, but a single point of failure.
                                                                      One misconfiguration can expose the internal
          Outsiders can reach services in the DMZ, but
                                                                      network.
          not walk straight into the internal network.                ,dNodhS" ir, kuq;a th ;ks ì|jeàï ,laIHhls'
          ndysr mqoa.,hskag DMZ ys we;s fiajdjka fj;                  tla jerÈ úkHdi lsÍulska wNHka;r cd,h

          we;=¿ úh fkdyel'

          the internal LAN.

          accept connections from the internet.
